## PART 1: EXECUTIVE SUMMARY & THE VALIDATED 80/20 HIGH-ROI PRIORITY MATRIX (`F7`)

### 1.1 Executive Thesis: Why These Four Creators Form a Complete System
Conventional fitness advice forces a false dichotomy: either follow a high-carbohydrate bodybuilding diet (300–450 g/day of carbohydrates) that chronically elevates insulin and forces painful bulk-and-cut cycles, or follow an unmeasured "lazy carnivore/keto" diet that neglects biomechanical resistance training and eventually hits a metabolic plateau.

When we synthesize the four target YouTube investigations alongside the broader clinical and scientific bodies of work from **Dr. Annette Bosworth, MD ("Dr. Boz")**, **Jeff Nippard**, **Jeremy Ethier**, and **Ryan Humiston**, a remarkable convergence emerges:
1. **Dr. Annette Bosworth ("Dr. Boz" — `eHorWI3mvcU`)** provides the **metabolic chemistry and circadian fasting engine**: proving that *"ketosis is blood chemistry, not just a carnivore food list,"* diagnosing post-honeymoon carnivore stalls via the **15-minute Morning Fasting Blood Test** and **Dr. Boz Ratio** ($\text{Glucose mg/dL} \div \text{Ketones mmol/L}$), protecting lean muscle mass against GLP-1/Ozempic starvation catabolism, and structuring meal timing along the **12-stage `ketoCONTINUUM`**.
2. **Jeff Nippard (`PiYSbR2B85w`)** provides the **macro-programming, volume, and effort architecture**: proving in a 365-day (14-month) DEXA and B-mode ultrasound experiment co-designed with **Dr. Stuart Phillips** that taking S-tier, high-stability exercises to **0–2 RIR (with the final set to true 0 RIR failure)** drives **+10.0 lbs of pure muscle gain and -10.0 lbs of fat loss** during high-body-fat recomposition (his brother Brad) and **+2.7 lbs of retained net contractile muscle at 14.5% body fat** even after 15 years of natural training (Jeff)—while proving that lean advanced lifters cannot "maingain" at maintenance calories.
3. **Jeremy Ethier (`0ShG8aVIvms` & `Baf5YrNvEDU`)** provides **anatomical sub-region biomechanics, EMG/MRI exercise validation, and daily NEAT anchoring**: testing 30 abdominal exercises across 31 chapters to prove that straight-leg raises spike hip-flexor interference by **+40%** unless the pelvis is curled into a C-shape, crowning the **Face-Away Lat Pulldown Cable Crunch**, **Reverse Crunch with AbMat**, and **Garhammer Raises** as S-tier winners, clarifying the 3 components of stretch-mediated hypertrophy via his $40,000 MRI study, and anchoring fat loss with **8,000–10,000+ daily steps**.
4. **Ryan Humiston (`jRbj25q-AjU`)** provides **forensic supplement auditing and high-tension isolation micro-biomechanics**: exposing BodyHealth's *Perfect Amino* ("99% NNU/AAU, 5 g EAA = 30 g protein / 1 calorie") as a thermodynamic fraud that violates conservation of mass, revealing why **NSF facility certification** does not validate efficacy claims, and engineering **constant-tension 3/4-ROM isolation finishers** that exhaust remaining muscle fibers at the end of a session without axial or CNS burnout.

### 1.4 Forensic Audit of Round 1 vs. Round 2 Video Counts Across All 4 Creators (`F11`)
> **User Follow-Up (`ORIGINAL_REQUEST.md`, 2026-11-07T16:08:20Z)**: *"How many videos of each of the content creators did you watch it seems like one when the goal was to digest their complete libraries"*

#### 1.4.1 Transparent Four-Tier Accounting: What Round 1 Covered vs. What Round 2 Delivers

### 2.2 Granular Breakdown of Video 1 (`F1`): `eHorWI3mvcU` — *"Dr. Boz: Stop Doing THIS on CARNIVORE"*
- **URL**: `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eHorWI3mvcU` (`https://youtu.be/eHorWI3mvcU?is=KvrNJYa8yMuPukoC`)
- **Channel / Creator**: **HomeSteadHow** (`https://www.youtube.com/@Homesteadhow`, hosted by Kerry Mann, producer of *Healing Humanity: The Power of a Proper Human Diet*), in a clinical interview with **Dr. Annette Bosworth, MD ("Dr. Boz")** (`@DoctorBoz`, `bozmd.com`).
- **Publication Date & Views**: Published **November 4, 2026** (`28,867 views`, `928+ likes`, `134 comments`).
- **Verbatim Video Description**:
  > *"Stalled on carnivore? Doing great, then the scale stops, or even goes up? Dr. Boz says she sees it all the time. I sat down with Dr. Annette Bosworth (Dr. Boz) to find out why it happens, and what she tells patients when it does."*

#### Complete 6-Chapter Timestamp & Physiological Breakdown
1. **`0:00` — Stuck After the Honeymoon**:
   - During the first **4 to 6 weeks** of adopting a carnivore or ketogenic diet ("the honeymoon phase"), cutting dietary carbohydrates produces dramatic results: falling insulin signals the renal tubules to dump excess retained sodium and extracellular edema (reducing joint pain, bloating, and blood pressure), fat-soluble steroid hormones trapped in adipose tissue are liberated, and an initial wave of circulating ketones boosts mental energy.
   - However, between **Month 2 and Month 18**, a large percentage of carnivores hit a hard metabolic wall: fat loss stops completely, visceral belly fat or scale weight creeps back up, and energy plateaus.
2. **`1:55` — Why Carnivore Stalls (The 4 Metabolic Traps)**:
   - **Trap 1: Staying in the High-Calorie "Gluttony Phase" Indefinitely**: Carnivore beginners are rightfully told to eat fatty meat until stuffed during the first 4–6 weeks to break sugar addiction and replenish cholesterol-derived hormones. However, continuing to graze on unlimited meat, butter, cheese, and pork rinds 3–5 times a day after Week 6 keeps hepatic energy flux saturated so the body never taps into its *own* stored adipose triglycerides.
   - **Trap 2: Circadian Melatonin–Insulin Mismatch (Eating After Sunset)**: Eating heavy carnivore dinners after dark—when pineal **melatonin** rises—impairs pancreatic beta-cell insulin regulation and keeps blood glucose and insulin elevated for **7 to 12+ hours overnight**. Instead of spending the night producing ketones and executing cellular repair (**autophagy**), the liver spends all night digesting dinner.
   - **Trap 3: Excess Protein Boluses & Powdered Protein Gluconeogenesis**: In metabolically damaged or insulin-resistant individuals, eating massive lean protein meals—or consuming hydrolyzed/isolate protein powders that **saturate 100% of upper-GI amino acid receptors instantaneously** (whereas whole steak saturates only **~30% of receptors gradually**)—triggers a substantial insulin surge and hepatic gluconeogenesis, shutting off ketogenesis.
   - **Trap 4: Hidden Dairy Carbs & Casein ("Casomorphins")**: Consuming milk, yogurt, kefir, cottage cheese, or commercial coffee creamers introduces hidden lactose sugars and bovine **casein**, which cleaves in the gut into morphine-like peptides (**casomorphins**) that perpetuate food cravings and keep insulin in double digits.
3. **`6:28` — The Morning Test ("The Morning Number Tattletales on You")**:
   - Dr. Boz states her foundational clinical rule: **Ketosis is a measurable blood chemistry, not a food list.** Eating zero-carb ribeye does not automatically mean your liver is making ketones.
   - Patients must perform a **paired fasting capillary blood test** (measuring both **Blood Glucose in mg/dL** and **Blood Beta-Hydroxybutyrate [BHB] in mmol/L** using a dual meter like Keto-Mojo) **within 15–30 minutes of waking ("feet on the floor")**, before coffee, exercise, or food.
   - Afternoon ketone tests are deceptive because skeletal muscle movement makes readings look artificially favorable; the **morning fasting test exposes what your liver and mitochondria did while you slept**:
     - **Stalled Carnivore Morning Signature**: Morning blood BHB sits on the floor at **$0.2\text{–}0.4\text{ mmol/L}$** while fasting morning glucose creeps into the prediabetic range (**$100\text{–}115+\text{ mg/dL}$**).
     - **Healing / Fat-Burning Morning Target**: Morning blood BHB of **$\ge 0.7\text{ mmol/L}$** (minimum $>0.5\text{ mmol/L}$) with fasting morning glucose settling below $100\text{ mg/dL}$ (ideally **$70\text{–}90\text{ mg/dL}$**).
4. **`7:51` — Her Ozempic / GLP-1 Agonist Warning & Clinical Micro-Dosing Context**:
   - Dr. Boz addresses the widespread use of GLP-1 receptor agonists (Ozempic/semaglutide, Wegovy, Mounjaro/tirzepatide—synthetic peptides modeled on Gila monster venom exendin-4 that resist DPP-4 degradation).
   - **The Catabolic Danger**: Pharmaceutical doses flood receptors at **$500\text{–}1,000\times$ natural physiological hormone levels**, silencing hypothalamic "food noise" and paralyzing gastric emptying. However, when a patient starves on Ozempic *without* a ketogenic metabolism (no circulating BHB to activate AMPK/autophagy and spare muscle) and *without* resistance training, **up to 25–40% of lost weight comes from lean skeletal muscle mass and bone mineral density**. Patients suffer **starvation sarcopenia**, inflammatory fat-cell necrosis ("hardened subcutaneous lumps"), loose **"skin curtains"** (because autophagy was never turned on to recycle excess dermal collagen/elastin), and rapid fat regain upon stopping the drug.
   - **Dr. Boz's Clinical Protocol**: Never use GLP-1s as a shortcut around metabolic repair. First, build a **"bed of ketones"** for **$\ge 21\text{ consecutive days}$** of strict ketosis to restore mitochondrial mitophagy. Only in cases of severe refractory food addiction/insulin resistance should a physician use a **compounded micro-dose** (**$1/5\text{th}$ down to $4\%$ of the standard pharmaceutical dose**, e.g., $0.6\text{ mg}$ tirzepatide) while verifying via daily morning blood tests that BHB remains $\ge 0.7\text{ mmol/L}$ and the Dr. Boz Ratio stays in the autophagy zone ($<40$) so lean muscle is preserved and skin tightens.
5. **`10:53` — How to Track It (The Dr. Boz Ratio & Press-Pulse Levers)**:
   - Divide morning fasting Glucose ($\text{mg/dL}$) by morning blood Ketones ($\text{mmol/L}$) to get the **Dr. Boz Ratio (DBR)**:
     - **$\text{DBR} \le 80$**: Nutritional ketosis & steady fat-loss zone ("not building chronic disease").
     - **$\text{DBR} \le 40$**: Deep metabolic repair, immune healing, skin tightening, and **autophagy** zone.
     - **$\text{DBR} \le 20$**: Intensive therapeutic ketosis (achieved during 36h–72h fasts for brain/oncology repair).
   - When a carnivore stalls (morning BHB $0.2\text{–}0.3\text{ mmol/L}$), pull **one metabolic stress lever at a time ("Press-Pulse Strategy")**:
     1. Compress the eating window (`ketoCONTINUUM` Stage 4 $\rightarrow$ Stage 8: 2MAD $\rightarrow$ 16:8 $\rightarrow$ Advanced 16:8 with black coffee $\rightarrow$ OMAD).
     2. Slide the eating window toward sunrise (**no calories for 12 hours before sunrise**).
     3. Execute a periodic **36-hour to 72-hour water/salt fast** or **72-Hour Sardine Fast** to shock mitochondria back into deep ketogenesis.
6. **`12:19` — Ask Dr. Boz (`askdrboz.com`)**:
   - Verbatim closing quote: *"Recently if you type in askdrboz.com, it's a new tool that we've just beta tested to say we have lots of videos out there, it can be hard to find your answers, uh it just kind of helps people connect to uh the resources of what we've taught, and it's free."*

### 2.3 Granular Breakdown of Video 2 (`F2`): `0ShG8aVIvms` — *"It's Weird, But Destroyed Every Ab Exercise I Tested"*
- **URL**: `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0ShG8aVIvms` (`https://youtu.be/0ShG8aVIvms?is=HGaHK0ktzcXrXoeS`)
- **Channel / Creator**: **Jeremy Ethier** (`https://www.youtube.com/@JeremyEthier`, Founder of *Built With Science*, 7.99M subscribers) with co-host Dennis.
- **Publication Date & Views**: Published **September 27, 2026** (`2,185,290 views`, duration `22:25`, 31 chapters).
- **Experimental Methodology**:
  - Tested **30 abdominal exercises** using **surface electromyography (EMG)** electrodes placed on the **upper rectus abdominis**, **lower rectus abdominis**, **obliques**, and **hip flexors (rectus femoris / iliopsoas)**.
  - Evaluated every movement against **4 scientific hypertrophy criteria**:
    1. **High regional abdominal fiber recruitment** (top-down thoracic flexion biases upper abs; bottom-up posterior pelvic tilt biases lower abs).
    2. **Minimal hip-flexor interference** (preventing the iliopsoas and rectus femoris from dominating the movement and straining the lumbar spine).
    3. **Deep mechanical tension in the lengthened (stretched) position** (allowing the spine and pelvis to extend **$15^\circ\text{–}30^\circ$ past neutral** at the bottom of every rep).
    4. **High external stability and seamless progressive overload** in the **8–15 rep hypertrophy range**.

#### Complete 31-Chapter Index & Biomechanical Results Across All 30 Exercises
| Group | Verbatim Chapters & Timestamps | EMG & Biomechanical Findings | Tier Rating |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Group 1: Floor & Lat-Pulldown Crunches** (`0:00–4:38`) | `0:00` Sit-Ups<br>`1:29` Crunches<br>`2:04` Crunches with Ab Mat<br>`2:41` Unassisted Lat Pulldown Crunch<br>`3:30` Assisted Lat Pulldown Crunch | **Sit-Ups (`0:00`)**: Flat floor blocks spinal extension (0° stretch); hip flexors dominate the second half. **Floor Crunches (`1:29`)**: Less hip-flexor takeover, but still zero stretch beyond neutral. **Crunches with AbMat / Rolled Towel (`2:04`)**: Lumbar contour allows $15^\circ\text{–}30^\circ$ spinal extension pre-stretch (**A-Tier**). **Unassisted Lat Pulldown Crunch (`2:41`)**: Great stretch, but too hard for most lifters (Dennis got 0 clean reps). **Assisted Lat Pulldown Crunch (`3:30`)**: Holding the rope in front for counterbalance allowed Dennis to perform 10 clean reps with a deep stretch (**S-Tier**). | Sit-Up: C<br>Crunch: B<br>AbMat Crunch: **A**<br>Assisted Lat Crunch: **S** |
| **Group 2: Leg Raise Variations** (`4:38–9:20`) | `4:38` Lying Leg Raises<br>`5:22` Hanging Leg Raises (Bent-Knee)<br>`5:44` Hanging Leg Raises (Straight Leg)<br>`6:28` Hanging Leg Raises (Curved Pelvis)<br>`7:27` Hanging Leg Raises (With Bar)<br>`8:39` Captain's Chair Leg Raise | **Lying (`4:38`) & Bent-Knee Hanging Leg Raises (`5:22`)**: Pivoting at the hip joint without curling the pelvis primarily trains hip flexors. **Straight-Leg Hanging Leg Raises (`5:44`)**: Made hip-flexor dominance *worse*—upper/lower ab EMG rose minimally while **hip-flexor EMG spiked by +40%**. **Curved Pelvis / "C-Shape" Cue (`6:28`)**: Initiating by posteriorly tilting/curling the pelvis upward into a C-shape strongly activates the lower abs. Adding a **Bar Behind the Back (`7:27`)** or **Captain's Chair (`8:39`)** eliminates momentum swinging and grip failure. | Straight-Leg (hip hinge): D<br>Curved Pelvis w/ Back Support or Captain's Chair: **A** |
| **Group 3: Isometrics & Calisthenics** (`9:20–13:56`) | `9:20` Plank<br>`9:59` RKC Plank<br>`10:50` Dragon Flag<br>`12:12` Front Lever Raises<br>`12:29` Garhammer Hold | **Standard Plank (`9:20`)**: Low EMG, zero stretch, zero dynamic ROM. **RKC Plank (`9:59`)**: Squeezing glutes/quads and pulling elbows toward toes **nearly tripled (~3x) ab EMG**, but still static. **Dragon Flag (`10:50`) & Front Lever (`12:12`)**: Produced massive raw EMG, but abs act as an isometric anti-extension brace rather than flexing dynamically from a stretch, and are too difficult for 95% of lifters. **Garhammer Hold (`12:29`)**: Hanging hips off a bench in the bottom stretched position placed intense long-muscle-length tension on the lower abs. | Plank: D<br>RKC Plank: B<br>Dragon Flag: B<br>Garhammer Hold: A- |
| **Group 4: Dynamic Lower-Ab Winners** (`13:56–15:51`) | `13:56` **Garhammer Raises**<br>`14:37` Reverse Crunch<br>`15:22` **Reverse Crunch With Ab Mat** | **Garhammer Raises (`13:56`)**: Turning the Garhammer Hold into dynamic pelvic curls (hips hanging off a flat/incline bench, knees bent in a frog/V-stance, starting with knees dropped below bench level in a deep lower-ab stretch, then curling the pelvis upward). Produced the **#1 highest lower-to-upper ab EMG ratio** of all 30 exercises, deep stretch, and easy progression via dumbbell between feet or steeper incline (**S-Tier Advanced Lower Abs**). **Reverse Crunch with AbMat (`15:22`)**: Placing an AbMat under the lower back lets the pelvis drop into extension at the bottom before curling the tailbone toward the sternum (**S-Tier Beginner/Intermediate Lower Abs**). | Garhammer Raise: **S (#1 Adv Lower)**<br>AbMat Reverse Crunch: **S (#1 Beg Lower)** |
| **Group 5: Home / Circuit Ab Moves** (`15:51–18:31`) | `15:51` Ab Roller<br>`16:53` Bicycle Crunches<br>`17:38` V-Ups<br>`17:45` Butterfly Kicks<br>`17:52` Mountain Climbers<br>`17:59` Dead Bugs | **Ab Roller (`15:51`)**: High anti-extension tension, but no spinal flexion or bottom extension stretch. **Bicycle Crunches (`16:53`), V-Ups (`17:38`), Butterfly Kicks (`17:45`), Mountain Climbers (`17:52`), Dead Bugs (`17:59`)**: Downgraded due to high hip-flexor involvement, lack of loaded bottom stretch, and inability to progressively overload in the 8–15 rep range. | Ab Roller: B+<br>Circuit Moves: C / D |
| **Group 6: Cable Crunches & Final Verdict** (`18:31–22:25`) | `18:31` Kneeling Cable Crunches<br>`19:17` Kneeling Cable Crunches (Facing Away)<br>`19:41` Standing Cable Crunches (Facing Away)<br>`19:56` Lat Pulldown Cable Crunch<br>`20:45` **Lat Pulldown Cable Crunch (Face-Away)**<br>`21:27` **Final Results** | **Kneeling Facing Stack (`18:31`)**: Poor resistance at top stretch; encourages hip-flexion sitting back onto heels. **Facing Away Kneeling/Standing (`19:17–19:56`)**: Fixes line of pull, but lacks back support. **Face-Away Lat Pulldown Cable Crunch (`20:45`)**: Sitting on the lat pulldown seat **facing away from the stack**, locking thighs under the pad, raising the pad against the upper back/scapulae as a pivot point, extending backward over the pad into a full thoracic stretch, and crunching ribs to pelvis. Maximum stability, deep loaded stretch, zero hip cheating, and 2.5–5 lb pin progression (**#1 S-Tier Upper Abs for Both Beginner & Advanced**). | Kneeling Facing Stack: B-<br>**Face-Away Lat Pulldown Cable Crunch: S+ (#1 Upper Abs)** |

- **Verbatim Final Verdict (`21:27–22:25`)**:
  > *"Based on Dennis and I's experience with each exercise, if I had to pick only two that would cover all your bases and be the best of the best options: for beginners, it would have to be the **face-away lat pulldown cable crunch** for the upper abs, and **reverse crunches with an AbMat** for the lower abs. Whereas for advanced, it would honestly still be the same exercise for the upper abs, but I'm going to have to go with **Garhammer raises** for the lower abs. That said, you can do a million reps of these, but your abs are never going to show if your diet isn't also locked in."*

- **URL**: `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PiYSbR2B85w` (`https://youtu.be/PiYSbR2B85w?is=Yd1f15dgMJEAm43u`)

  - Co-designed by **Jeff Nippard**, **Alysha D'Souza**, **Derrick Van Every**, and **Dr. Stuart Phillips** (McMaster University); research assistance by Max Edsey.
  - **Primary Peer-Reviewed Citations in Video Description**:
    1. *Proximity to Failure*: **Robinson ZP, Pelland JC, Remmert JF, Refalo MC, Jukic I, Steele J, Zourdos MC (2024)**, *Sports Medicine*, 54(9):2209–2231 (`PMID: 38970765` / `PMID: 38970922`).

#### Complete Experimental Design, Training Protocol & Phase-by-Phase DEXA Results
- **Subjects & Baseline Measurements (Serial DEXA Scans + B-Mode Ultrasound + Isokinetic Dynamometry)**:
  - **Subject 1 — Jeff Nippard (Advanced Natural Lifter)**: 15+ years of serious drug-free training, 5'5", starting at **$165.0\text{ lbs}$** and **$15.8\%$ body fat**. Ambitious 1-year goal: **$+4.0\text{ lbs}$** of pure lean mass.
  - **Subject 2 — Brad Nippard (Untrained Biological Brother)**: Starting at **$165.0\text{ lbs}$** and **$33\text{–}36\%$ body fat**. 1-year goal: **$+9.0\text{ lbs}$** of pure lean mass.
- **Training Split & The 3 Non-Negotiable Rules**:
  - **5-Day Hybrid ULPPL Split**: **2 Strength-Biased Sessions (Upper, Lower)** + **3 Hypertrophy-Biased Sessions (Push, Pull, Legs)** + **2 Rest Days/Week** (0 missed workouts across 365+ days).
  - **Rule 1 (Effort / Proximity to Failure)**: Take early sets to 1–2 RIR and push the **last working set of every single exercise to true momentary muscular failure (0 RIR)**.
  - **Rule 2 (S-Tier Stretch-Biased Exercise Selection)**: Restrict exercises to high-stability, stretch-loaded movements (45° Incline Barbell Press, Seated Cable Flye, Weighted Pull-Up, Chest-Supported T-Bar Row, Deficit Pendlay Row with lengthened partials, High-Pulley Cable Lateral Raise, Overhead Cable Triceps Extension, Bayesian Cable Curl, Preacher Curl, Hack Squat, Seated Leg Curl).
  - **Rule 3 (Strict Progressive Overload + Sleep/Supplement Hygiene)**: Log every set/rep weekly, track sleep regularity via **Oura Rings**, and use 8 evidence-based supplements (Creatine monohydrate, Protein powder, Caffeine, Fish oil, Magnesium, Vitamin D, Multivitamin, Ashwagandha).

| Experimental Phase | Caloric & Macro Protocol (Jeff) | Jeff Nippard (Advanced, 15 Yrs Lifting) Outcome | Brad Nippard (Untrained Brother, 33–36% BF) Outcome | Key Scientific Lesson for Our Program |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1-Month Check-In** | $3,000\text{ kcal/day}$ ($+15\%$ surplus) | **$+3.0\text{ lbs}$ DEXA LBM** | **$+12.7\text{ lbs}$ DEXA LBM**, **$-3.0\%$ body fat** | Early DEXA lean mass jumps include rapid intracellular water and glycogen expansion from creatine + novel mechanical tension. |

### 2.5 Granular Breakdown of Video 4 (`F4`): `jRbj25q-AjU` — *"Exposing The Biggest Scam In Supplements"*
- **URL**: `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jRbj25q-AjU` (`https://youtu.be/jRbj25q-AjU?is=R1guU9iC7107XS6f`)
- **Channel / Creator**: **Ryan Humiston** (`https://www.youtube.com/@RyanHumiston`, 2.23M subscribers).
- **Publication Date & Metrics**: Published **October 13, 2026** (`614,436 views`, `10K+ likes`, duration `16:39`).
- **Verbatim Video Description**:
  > *"I spent the last month diving into the claims I see every day on my feed surrounding perfect amino, and I was not ready for what I found... I assumed the numbers were exaggerated for marketing purposes, but I had no clue that the science behind it was riddled with lies, that the owner of Bodyhealth was a Scientologist with a dark past, and that even when your supplement says NSF, it doesn't mean anything. Enjoy!"*

#### Complete 8-Chapter Timestamp & Forensic Deconstruction
1. **`0:00` — Introduction to the Scam**:
   - Investigates **Perfect Amino** (manufactured by **BodyHealth**, founded by **Dr. David Minkoff**, and heavily promoted across biohacking, keto, and fasting podcasts by **Gary Brecka** and John Jaquish).
2. **`1:20` — Analyzing the 99% Claim**:
   - BodyHealth and its affiliate influencers claim that **1 serving ($5\text{ g}$ of 8 crystalline free-form essential amino acids [EAAs], labeled as "1–2 calories" with zero glucose/insulin impact) builds as much muscle protein as $29\text{–}30\text{ g}$ of whey protein or steak**.
   - They allege that Perfect Amino achieves **"99% Amino Acid Utilization (AAU) / Net Nitrogen Utilization (NNU)"**, while claiming whole meats/eggs have only $32\text{–}48\%$ utilization and whey protein has only $16\text{–}18\%$ utilization (falsely claiming $82\text{–}84\%$ of whey protein "turns into toxic sugar/glucose and nitrogen waste").
3. **`2:46` — Deconstructing the Research & `6:26` — Flaws in Nitrogen Testing**:
   - Humiston traces the entire marketing pyramid back to a heavily criticized 1998 paper by **Dr. Maurizio Lucà-Moretti** on the *"Master Amino Acid Pattern (MAP)"*.
   - **The Urinary Urea Fallacy**: Lucà-Moretti never performed muscle biopsies, DEXA scans, or stable-isotope tracer infusions ($L\text{-[ring-}^{13}\text{C}_6\text{]phenylalanine}$) to measure actual myofibrillar fractional synthesis rates (FSR). Instead, he fed protein-restricted subjects a tiny $5\text{ g}$ dose of free-form EAAs and measured acute **urinary urea nitrogen excretion**. Because $5\text{ g}$ is a tiny sub-threshold bolus in a depleted gut, the splanchnic bed (intestinal mucosa and liver) absorbs the free amino acids without an immediate rise in urinary urea—which Lucà-Moretti fraudulently equated to *"99% conversion into structural skeletal muscle protein,"* while ignoring fecal, dermal, and metabolic nitrogen pools.
   - Furthermore, **Non-Essential Amino Acids (NEAAs)** abundant in steak and eggs (glycine, proline, glutamine, arginine, alanine, cysteine) are **not "sugar waste"**—they are mandatory structural building blocks of human muscle (~50% of myofibrillar protein mass consists of NEAAs), collagen/tendons, glutathione antioxidant defense, and nitric oxide synthesis.
4. **`8:26` — Mathematical Inconsistencies (Violation of Conservation of Mass)**:
   - Humiston applies elementary physics and stoichiometry: **You cannot synthesize $29\text{–}30\text{ grams}$ of dry skeletal muscle protein out of $5\text{ grams}$ of amino acid powder.**
   - Even accounting for intracellular water in live muscle (~75% water, ~20% protein), synthesizing the contractile actin and myosin equivalent to $30\text{ g}$ of dietary protein requires **$30\text{ g}$ of physical amino acid mass**. While $3\text{–}5\text{ g}$ of leucine/EAAs can flip the biochemical ignition switch (**mTORC1 phosphorylation**), it does not supply the physical bricks and mortar—and during a fast, that mTORC1 spike **directly inhibits ULK1 and shuts off cellular autophagy**!
5. **`10:23` — Marketing vs. Science & `12:25` — Questionable Business Practices**:
   - Exposes how influencers repeat identical scripted talking points for 20–40% affiliate commissions, and documents the regulatory history of BodyHealth founder **Dr. David Minkoff** (a senior Scientologist whose medical license was suspended for a year and who settled a malpractice lawsuit after prescribing sedatives [Valium and chloral hydrate] without a physical examination to Scientologist **Lisa McPherson** prior to her 1995 dehydration/neglect death at the Fort Harrison Hotel).
6. **`14:38` — Certification and Conclusion (The NSF Certification Myth)**:
   - When confronted with scientific flaws, promoters hide behind the **"NSF Certified"** badge on the bottle. Humiston searched NSF's public database of revoked certifications and discovered **only ONE company in history had ever had its NSF certification revoked**—and only because it **mislabeled sulfuric acid as a municipal fluoride water treatment in Michigan**, corroding equipment and endangering public water (and even that company was re-certified by September 2026!).
   - **Lesson**: NSF certification only checks that a facility follows basic contaminant/banned-substance manufacturing rules; **it never validates whether a supplement's marketing claims work**.

### 2.6 Broader Core Methodologies of All 4 Creators (`F5`)

#### A. Dr. Annette Bosworth ("Dr. Boz"): Clinical Internal Medicine & Metabolic Repair
- **Clinical Foundation**: Board-certified Internal Medicine physician (20+ years experience), author of *Anyway You Can* (2018, documenting how a strict ketogenic protocol reduced her 71-year-old mother Grandma Rose's stage IV chronic lymphocytic leukemia [CLL] markers by 70% in 6 weeks) and *ketoCONTINUUM* (2020/2021).
- **Core Tenets**:
  1. **Chronic Hyperinsulinemia is the Root of Modern Disease**: Decades before HbA1c rises, high insulin locks fat inside adipocytes (inhibiting hormone-sensitive lipase), causes hypertension via renal sodium retention and sympathetic vasoconstriction, and floods tissues with microscopic edema ("cellular grime" measurable via the **shin thumbprint edema test**).
  2. **"Ketones Rise First, Glucose Falls Second"**: Beta-Hydroxybutyrate (**BHB**) is both a clean mitochondrial fuel and an epigenetic signaling molecule (HDAC inhibitor and **NLRP3 inflammasome inhibitor**) that suppresses ghrelin, calms neuro-inflammation (GABA > glutamate), and triggers **AMPK-mediated autophagy**.
  3. **Whole Meat vs. Powdered Protein Receptor Chemistry (`a-guide-to-fat-protein-and-chemistry`)**: 1 scoop of whey/isolate protein powder fills **100% of upper-digestive amino acid receptors immediately**, triggering an acute insulin surge and hepatic gluconeogenesis in insulin-resistant individuals. In contrast, **20 oz of whole steak fills only ~30% of upper-GI receptors gradually** over hours, providing abundant **leucine** to protect muscle mass against sarcopenia while keeping insulin flat.
  4. **Sequential Progression Along the 12-Stage `ketoCONTINUUM` & The 72-Hour Sardine Fast**: Moving systematically from $<20\text{ g}$ TOTAL carbs (`Stage 2`) through 2MAD (`Stage 4`), Advanced 16:8 (`Stage 6`), Sunrise-Aligned OMAD (`Stage 8`), and periodic 36h–72h autophagy/sardine fasts (`Stages 9–12`).

#### B. Jeff Nippard: Science-Based Hypertrophy, Volume Landmarks & *The Muscle Ladder*
- **Core Tenets**:
  1. **High-Efficiency Volume Landmarks (`DzjWEn2BS_k`)**: While classic meta-analyses (Schoenfeld et al., 2017) show a dose-response up to **10–20 sets/muscle/week**, Nippard's 2025–2026 evolution (*"I Cut The Number Of Sets I Do In Half"*) demonstrates that when sets are taken to **0–1 RIR** with **2.5–3+ minute rests** and zero junk volume, **6–12 hard sets/muscle/week** (2–3 sets per exercise) produces ~85–95% of maximal growth with half the joint and CNS wear.
  2. **Bi-Articulate Stretch-Mediated Hypertrophy & Post-Failure Lengthened Partials**: Prioritizing exercises that load bi-articulate muscles across both joints in their stretched position (**Seated Leg Curl** > Lying Leg Curl; **Overhead Cable Triceps Extension** > Pushdown; **Bayesian Curl**; **Standing Straight-Knee Calf Raise**), using a **2–4 second eccentric**, a **0.5–1 second pause at full stretch**, and adding **3–5 bottom-half lengthened partials** immediately after reaching full-ROM concentric failure on the final set (Wolf et al., 2025).
  3. **Double Progression & Deload Periodization**: Prescribing a rep range (e.g., 3 sets of 8–10 reps), holding load constant until all sets hit the top of the range at target RIR, then adding 2.5–5% weight; scheduling a **1-week Deload** every 6–8 weeks (-40% sets, -10–15% load, 3–4 RIR).

#### C. Jeremy Ethier (*Built With Science*): Anatomical Sub-Region Biomechanics & The $40K MRI Stretch Study
- **Core Tenets**:
  1. **Anatomical Line-of-Pull Alignment**: Matching cable/resistance vectors to specific regional muscle fibers:
     - *Chest*: **Clavicular head** via 15–30° Incline Press / Low-to-High Cable Flye (45–60° elbow tuck); **Sternocostal head** via Flat Converging Press / Horizontal Seated Cable Flye (crossing midline); **Costal/Lower head** via High-to-Low Cable Crossover / Forward-Leaning Dips.
     - *Back*: **Thoracic Lats** via Wide/Neutral Pulldown; **Iliac (Lower) Lats** via Half-Kneeling 1-Arm Sagittal Cable Pull-Around; **Mid-Back (Rhomboids/Traps)** via 45–60° Elbow-Flare Chest-Supported Row; **Lower Traps** via 120–135° Incline/Cable Y-Raises.
     - *Delts*: **Lateral head** in the **30° scapular plane** via Wrist-Height Behind-the-Back Cuffed Cable Lateral Raises; **Posterior head** at a **45° arm-to-torso angle** via Reverse Cable Crossovers.
  2. **The Landmark 2025–2026 $40,000 MRI Stretch Study (`Baf5YrNvEDU` — *We Need To Talk About "The Stretch" ft. Jeff Nippard*)**: Ethier funded a randomized MRI-measured hypertrophy study that separated "the stretch" into 3 distinct variables:
     - **Variable 1 (Bi-Articulate Muscle Length)**: Stretching a two-joint muscle across both joints (e.g., Seated Leg Curl, Overhead Triceps, Incline/Bayesian Curl, Straight-Leg Calf Raise) **IS proven superior**.
     - **Variable 2 (Full Range of Motion into the Bottom Stretch)**: Going all the way into the bottom stretch **IS proven superior** to cutting ROM short.
     - **Variable 3 (Stretch-Biased vs. Squeeze-Biased Resistance Profile when Full ROM & Effort Are Matched)**: When subjects used full ROM and trained to failure, a stretch-peaked cable profile vs. a mid/shortened-peaked profile produced **no statistically significant difference in whole-muscle MRI growth**—freeing lifters to use both stretch-loaded compounds and contraction-peaked exercises!

#### D. Ryan Humiston: Micro-Biomechanical Isolation & Constant-Tension Metabolic Finishers
- **Core Tenets**:
  1. **"Locking the Muscle in a Cage of Constant Tension" (3/4 ROM Rule)**: On movements where full joint lockout transfers load onto stacked bones and ligaments (e.g., locking knees at the top of squats/lunges/leg presses or resting dumbbells against the thighs on lateral raises), Humiston reverses direction just before lockout to keep intramuscular occlusion and tension continuous.
  2. **Micro-Adjustments in Joint Angles**: Driving elbows/biceps inward toward the sternum on chest presses (internal humeral adduction); supinating/reverse-gripping shoulder presses to stay in the scapular plane; pinning elbows into the ribs ("Poor Man's Preacher") or forward on Spider Curls; dragging the bar up the torso on Drag Curls; and keeping a slight forward torso lean with heel/midfoot pressure on 3/4-ROM lunges.
  3. **Strategic Metabolic Stress Finishers**: While a full 60-minute workout of Humiston 20-rep giant sets is counterproductive in ketosis (causing excessive glycogen depletion and cortisol/gluconeogenesis), deploying **1 final Humiston Constant-Tension Set (15–25 reps to 0 RIR + dropset/partials)** at the end of a muscle group's work recruits every remaining muscle fiber with zero axial fatigue.

### 3.2 Resolving the 5 Core "Keto vs. Hypertrophy" Tensions via Peer-Reviewed Science (`F6`)

#### Tension 1: Can You Build Equal Muscle & Maximal Strength in Strict Ketosis?
- **The Apparent Conflict**: Traditional bodybuilding programs prescribe $4\text{–}6\text{ g/kg/day}$ of carbohydrates under the assumption that exogenous carbs and high insulin are required to synthesize muscle protein and fuel lifting sets.
- **The Peer-Reviewed Resolution**:
  - Mechanical tension activates **mTORC1** directly via **focal adhesion kinase (FAK)** and intracellular **phosphatidic acid**—completely independent of carbohydrate-driven insulin spikes.
  - Furthermore, when resistance-trained lifters transition to a ketogenic diet, each gram of stored muscle/liver glycogen binds **$\sim 3\text{–}4\text{ grams}$ of intracellular water**. Early studies that measured DEXA Fat-Free Mass (FFM) *without* accounting for this initial $1.5\text{–}2.2\text{ kg}$ glycogen/water shift mistook benign water diuresis for "muscle loss."

##### Complete Summary Table of Controlled Ketogenic Resistance Training Trials & Meta-Analyses
| Study & PubMed/PMC ID | Design & Population | Dietary & Protein Protocol | Hypertrophy & Strength Outcomes | Key Mechanistic Takeaway |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Wilson et al. (2017 / 2020)**<br>*J Strength Cond Res*<br>`PMID: 28399015` / `32271225` | 11-week RCT in 25 resistance-trained men ($N=13$ KD, $N=12$ Western Diet) on periodized resistance training | Isocaloric, nitrogen-matched at **$1.7\text{ g/kg/day}$ protein**; KD: 5% CHO, 75% fat, 20% protein (mean blood **BHB $1.0\text{ mmol/L}$**) | **100% Equal 1RM Squat, Bench Press & Myofibrillar Hypertrophy**: At Week 10 (in ketosis), LBM rose $+2.4\%$ (KD) vs $+4.4\%$ (WD); at Week 11 (after equalizing intracellular glycogen/water), KD LBM rose **$+4.8\%$ ($+3.3\text{ kg}$)** vs $+4.4\%$ in WD, while KD lost significantly more fat (**$-2.2\text{ kg}$ vs $-1.5\text{ kg}$**). | Proves **true contractile muscle hypertrophy in ketosis is identical to a high-carb diet** when protein ($\ge 1.7\text{ g/kg}$) and calories are matched. |

To bulk (rather than cut), lean lifters must deliberately hit a $+150\text{–}300\text{ kcal}$ animal-fat surplus. |

#### Tension 2: High Protein ($1.6\text{–}2.2\text{ g/kg}$) vs. Demand-Driven Gluconeogenesis & Deep Ketosis
- **The Apparent Conflict**: In `eHorWI3mvcU`, Dr. Boz notes that insulin-resistant carnivores who eat excessive lean protein or whey powders all day can stall ketosis via gluconeogenesis (GNG). Meanwhile, Nippard, Ethier, and Humiston require **$1.6\text{–}2.2\text{ g/kg/day}$** of protein to maximize hypertrophy.
- **The Peer-Reviewed Resolution**:
  1. **Why $1.62\text{–}2.2\text{ g/kg/day}$ Is Non-Negotiable for Hypertrophy (Morton et al., 2018, `PMC5867436`)**: Across 49 RCTs ($N=1,863$), segmental linear regression proved that fat-free mass gains plateau at **$1.62\text{ g/kg/day}$**, with the upper 95% confidence bound at **$2.2\text{ g/kg/day}$** (adding $+2.49\text{ kg}$ 1RM strength, $+0.75\text{ kg}$ FFM in trained lifters, and $+310\ \mu\text{m}^2$ fiber cross-sectional area).
  2. **Double-Isotope Tracer Proof That Only 7.7% of Dietary Protein Turns Into Glucose (Fromentin et al., 2013, *Diabetes*, `PMID: 23274906` / `PMC3636601`)**:
     - Human subjects fasted overnight received intravenous `[6,6-2H2]`-glucose infusions and consumed whole eggs containing $23\text{ g}$ of intrinsically doubly labeled **`[15N]-[13C]`-protein** with **zero carbohydrates** ("optimal gluconeogenic conditions").
     - Over the 8-hour postprandial period, total endogenous glucose production (EGP) was **$50.4 \pm 7.7\text{ g}$**, of which **only $3.9 \pm 0.7\text{ g}$ ($7.7\%$) originated from the ingested dietary amino acids**! Over **80% of dietary amino acids escaped hepatic deamination** and were retained for tissue protein synthesis.
     - Biochemically, hepatic gluconeogenesis is **demand-driven** (regulated by PEPCK, Fructose-1,6-bisphosphatase, and Glucose-6-phosphatase) and costs **6 ATP molecules per molecule of glucose synthesized from pyruvate**. The liver does not wastefully burn 6 ATP to turn expensive amino acids into excess blood sugar when resistance-trained muscles are actively pulling those amino acids out of circulation via LAT1/mTORC1.
  3. **The Hepatic Insulin-to-Glucagon Ratio ($I:G$) Preserves Ketogenesis (Bikman, 2017/2023)**:
     - High-carbohydrate meals spike insulin while **suppressing pancreatic alpha-cell glucagon**, driving the $I:G$ ratio from a fasted baseline of $\sim 0.8$ up to **$>4.0$**, which increases malonyl-CoA, inhibits carnitine palmitoyltransferase-1 (CPT-1), and halts ketogenesis.
     - Conversely, in a carbohydrate-restricted ($<20\text{ g/d}$) state, whole animal protein stimulates both a moderate insulin pulse (to drive muscle amino acid uptake) **and a proportional glucagon rise** ($\sim 70\text{ pg/mL} \rightarrow \sim 140\text{ pg/mL}$). The hepatic **$I:G$ ratio remains low ($\sim 1.0\text{–}1.3$)**, keeping hepatocyte CPT-1 and ketogenesis active.
  4. **How We Satisfy Both Dr. Boz and Nippard/Ethier/Humiston Simultaneously**:
     - **Rule A (Calculate Protein on Ideal / Reference Body Weight)**: Set protein at **$1.6\text{–}2.2\text{ g/kg}$ of ideal/target body weight** (or $2.2\text{–}2.5\text{ g/kg}$ of lean body mass), never obese scale weight.
     - **Rule B (Eat Whole Animal Meat/Eggs, Never Rapid Whey Hydrolysate)**: As Dr. Boz explains (`a-guide-to-fat-protein-and-chemistry`), whole steak saturates only **~30% of upper-GI amino acid receptors gradually**, preventing the rapid portal vein insulin spike caused by protein powders.
     - **Rule C (Co-Ingest Animal Fat at a $1:1$ to $1.5:1$ Gram Ratio Inside 2MAD)**: Pairing every $70\text{–}95\text{ g}$ protein meal with $70\text{–}120\text{ g}$ of animal fat (ribeye, $80/20$ ground beef, egg yolks, butter, tallow, sardines) slows gastric emptying via CCK and supplies abundant free fatty acids for hepatic BHB synthesis, keeping morning BHB at **$0.8\text{–}2.5\text{ mmol/L}$** and the **Dr. Boz Ratio $<80$ (or $<40$)**.

---

#### Tension 3: Muscle Glycogen Resynthesis & Intracellular "Pump" Without Carbohydrates
- **The Apparent Conflict**: Lifters transitioning to keto often report feeling "flat" or losing 1–2 reps on sets 3–4 during their first 2 weeks.
- **The Peer-Reviewed Resolution**:
  1. **Resistance Training Depletes Only 24–40% of Local Glycogen**: Muscle biopsy studies (Tesch et al., 1986; MacDougall et al., 1999; Koopman et al., 2006) prove that a full hypertrophy workout depletes local muscle glycogen by only **24–40%**, not 80–100%.
  2. **Keto-Adapted Muscle Resynthesizes Glycogen at the Exact Same Rate via Lactate & Glycerol Recycling (Volek et al., 2016 FASTER Study, *Metabolism*, `PMID: 26892521`)**:
     - Volek and Phinney performed serial vastus lateralis muscle biopsies on elite keto-adapted athletes ($<10\%$ CHO, $70\%$ fat, $19\%$ protein for 9–20 months) vs. high-carb athletes ($59\%$ CHO).
     - Keto-adapted athletes oxidized fat at **2.3x the rate** of high-carb athletes (**$1.54 \pm 0.18\text{ g/min}$ vs $0.67 \pm 0.14\text{ g/min}$**, $p=0.000$, with peak fat oxidation at **$70.3\%$ of $\text{VO}_2\text{max}$**), yet maintained **identical resting muscle glycogen ($\sim 140\text{ mmol/kg wet weight}$)**, identical exercise depletion (**64%**), and **identical 2-hour post-exercise glycogen resynthesis (**36% vs 34%**) without consuming any carbohydrates**!
     - **Mechanism**: During intense lifting sets, anaerobic glycolysis generates **lactate**, while catecholamine-stimulated adipose lipolysis splits each triglyceride into 3 fatty acids + **1 glycerol backbone**. The liver recycles circulating **lactate (via the Cori cycle)** and **glycerol (via glycerol kinase $\rightarrow$ DHAP)** into glucose, which exercised muscles rapidly absorb via insulin-independent **contraction-mediated GLUT4 translocation** to refill intramuscular glycogen.
  3. **Why "Flat Muscles" Are Actually Renal Sodium Loss—and How Pre-Workout Hyperhydration Fixes It (DeFronzo et al., 1975/1981; Haussinger et al., 1996)**:
     - Low fasting insulin reduces renal NHE3/ENaC sodium reabsorption, causing the kidneys to excrete an extra **2,000–3,000 mg of sodium per day** ("natriuresis of fasting", `PMC11978633`) and reducing plasma volume by 8–12%.
     - By ingesting **1,000–1,500 mg Sodium (Redmond Real Salt) + 5 g Creatine Monohydrate + 2–3 g L-Taurine (+ optional 6–8 g L-Citrulline) in 500–750 mL water 30–45 minutes pre-workout**, plasma volume expands acutely by ~8–10% and water is pulled *inside* the myofibril (**intracellular cell swelling**, which Haussinger et al., 1996 proved independently activates integrin/MAPK anabolic signaling), restoring explosive bar speed and skin-splitting muscle pumps with zero insulin spike.

---

#### Tension 4: Training Volume, Rest Intervals & Cortisol Control in Ketosis
- **The Apparent Conflict**: High-volume "pump" programs (25–30 sets/muscle with 30–60s rest, or full workouts of Humiston 20-rep giant sets) cause excessive glycogen depletion and trigger an **HPA-axis cortisol and epinephrine surge** that stimulates hepatic glycogenolysis/gluconeogenesis, spiking blood glucose ($>130\text{ mg/dL}$) and crashing blood ketones.
- **The Peer-Reviewed Resolution**:
  1. **2–3 Minute Rest Intervals Maximize Both Hypertrophy and Aerobic $\text{PCr}$ Recovery (Schoenfeld et al., 2016, `PMID: 26605807`; Grgic et al., 2018, `PMID: 28933024`)**:
     - Resting **2.5–3.0 minutes** between compound sets allows **90–95% mitochondrial oxidative resynthesis of phosphocreatine ($\text{PCr}$)** fueled entirely by fatty acids and BHB, producing significantly greater quad/triceps hypertrophy and 1RM strength than 1-minute rest intervals while preventing glycolytic stress.
  2. **10–16 Hard Sets/Muscle/Week at 0–2 RIR Eliminates Junk Volume (Schoenfeld et al., 2017, `PMID: 27433992`; Robinson/Refalo et al., 2024, `PMID: 38970765`; Wolf et al., 2025)**:
     - By combining **S-tier stretch-loaded exercises**, **2–4s controlled eccentrics**, **0–2 RIR effort** (final set to 0 RIR + 3–5 lengthened partials), and **1 final Humiston constant-tension finisher set**, lifters achieve 100% of maximal mechanical tension in **10–16 weekly sets** (45–60 min sessions) without triggering cortisol spillover.

#### Tension 5: Synchronizing Intermittent Fasting (16:8 / 2MAD / OMAD / 36–72h Fasts) with Muscle Protein Synthesis
- **The Apparent Conflict**: Classic sports nutrition claimed the body could only absorb or utilize 25–30 g of protein per meal, requiring 4–6 meals/day—which directly contradicts Dr. Boz's `ketoCONTINUUM` (`eHorWI3mvcU`).
- **The Peer-Reviewed Resolution**:
  1. **Single 100 g Whole-Protein Boluses Sustain Anabolism for $>12\text{ Hours}$ (Trommelen et al., 2023, *Cell Reports Medicine*)**:
     - Dr. Luc van Loon's landmark human isotope study proved there is **no upper limit to single-meal anabolic protein utilization**: ingesting $100\text{ g}$ of whole protein in a single meal produces a prolonged, dose-dependent increase in plasma amino acid availability and **myofibrillar protein synthesis lasting $>12\text{ hours}$** while suppressing whole-body proteolysis.
  2. **16:8 Time-Restricted Feeding Matches Traditional Eating for Hypertrophy While Accelerating Fat Loss (Moro et al., 2016, `PMID: 27737674`; Tinsley et al., 2019, `PMID: 31268131`)**:
     - Both 8-week RCTs proved that resistance-trained men and women eating inside an 8-hour window (16:8) maintained or gained lean mass and 1RM strength identically to normal feeding while losing significantly more fat (**-1.62 kg**) and lowering fasting insulin and inflammatory cytokines.
  3. **Synchronizing 36–72h Autophagy Fasts with Training Deloads**:
     - On standard training weeks (Weeks 1–6), use **16:8 or 18:6 Two-Meal-A-Day (2MAD)** delivering **70–95 g of whole animal protein in Meal 1 and Meal 2** (totaling 140–190 g/day).
     - Schedule Dr. Boz's **36-hour to 72-hour Autophagy Fasts** (or **72-Hour Sardine Fasts**, targeting **Dr. Boz Ratio $<20$**) during **Week 7 Training Deload Weeks**, when training volume is reduced by 40–50% and elevated BHB ($2.0\text{–}4.0+\text{ mmol/L}$) + surged Growth Hormone ($300\text{–}500\%$, Cahill 1970 / Nair et al. 1988) actively spare skeletal muscle tissue!

## PART 4: THE SUPER KETOVORE DIET PROTOCOL (`F8`)

### 4.1 Tiered Super Ketovore Food Lists ("Good, Better, BEST" vs. Strict Exclusions) (`F8`)
Why **"Super Ketovore"** outperforms both standard "Dirty Keto" and unmeasured "Blind Carnivore":
- **Standard "Dirty" Keto Fails** because "Net Carb" math, commercial keto bars/breads, nut flours, artificial sweeteners, and industrial seed oils keep postprandial insulin elevated and trigger sweet cravings.
- **Blind Carnivore Stalls** after 3–12 months (`eHorWI3mvcU`) when adherents graze on unlimited calories late into the night, drink casein-laden dairy/creamers, or consume excessive lean protein without monitoring morning blood BHB ($0.2\text{–}0.4\text{ mmol/L}$).
- **The Super Ketovore Solution**: Anchor **90–100% of daily nutrition in Tier 1 nutrient-dense animal proteins and fats**, optionally include **$\le 10\%$ Tier 2 low-toxicity fermented/omega-3 enhancers**, and enforce a strict **$\le 20\text{ g}$ TOTAL carbohydrate ceiling** (never subtracting fiber or sugar alcohols).

| **TIER 1: Core Super Ketovore Foundation** *(90–100% of Daily Intake)* | **Ruminant & Fatty Meats** | Ribeye steak, NY strip, chuck roast, beef brisket, $80/20$ or $75/25$ ground beef, lamb chops/ground lamb, bison, venison, pork belly, sugar-free uncured bacon. | Delivers complete essential + non-essential amino acids, high **L-leucine** ($>3\text{–}4\text{ g}$ per meal) for mTORC1 activation with slow ~30% upper-GI receptor saturation, plus natural **creatine** ($\sim 2\text{ g/lb}$), **carnosine**, **L-taurine**, **L-carnitine**, heme iron, zinc, and B12. |
| **TIER 1: Core Super Ketovore Foundation** | **Pasture-Raised Whole Eggs (Prioritizing Yolks)** | Whole pasture-raised chicken/duck eggs and extra egg yolks cooked in butter, ghee, or tallow. | As Dr. Boz emphasizes, egg whites are mostly water and lean albumin, whereas the **egg yolk** houses 100% of the fat-soluble vitamins ($A, D_3, E, K_2$), **choline** (for hepatic VLDL export and acetylcholine neurotransmission), biotin, folate, and phospholipids. |
| **TIER 1: Core Super Ketovore Foundation** | **Small Wild Fatty Fish ("The Sardine Queen" Staples)** | Canned **wild sardines** (in water, brine, or olive oil), wild herring, Atlantic mackerel, anchovies, wild-caught sockeye/king salmon, oysters, salmon roe. | Rich in bioavailable phospholipid/triglyceride **EPA & DHA Omega-3s**, Vitamin D3, selenium, CoQ10, and bioavailable calcium (from soft edible sardine bones). Powerfully suppresses ghrelin and lowers the NLRP3 inflammasome. |

Switch from urine strips to **daily morning fingerstick blood Glucose & BHB testing**. | Week 4 Transition Milestone. |

Eliminates digesting food while melatonin is elevated, curing stubborn morning Dawn Phenomenon glucose spikes. | Stall-Breaker & Rest-Day Protocol. |

#### The 72-Hour Sardine Fast ("Press-Pulse" Stall-Breaker Protocol)
When a carnivore/ketovore hits a stubborn fat-loss plateau or morning ketones drop to $0.2\text{–}0.4\text{ mmol/L}$ (`eHorWI3mvcU`):
1. **Protocol**: For **72 consecutive hours**, consume **only canned wild sardines** (packed in water, brine, or olive oil; seasoned only with salt, black pepper, mustard, or hot sauce), **water**, **black coffee/plain tea**, **$5,000\text{–}6,000\text{ mg/day}$ sodium (Redmond Real Salt)**, and **evening chelated magnesium**. Eat whenever genuinely hungry (typically $3\text{–}6\text{ cans/day}$).
2. **Why It Works**: Eliminates all hidden dairy lactose/casein, delivers pure marine EPA/DHA + bioavailable protein + fat with zero insulin spike, triggers sensory-specific satiety (eliminating hedonic "foodie" overeating), drains intercellular edema (verifiable via the **shin thumbprint test**), and drives blood BHB to **$2.0\text{–}4.0+\text{ mmol/L}$** ($\text{DBR} < 30$) without sacrificing lean muscle mass.

### 4.4 Morning Biomarker Testing & The Dr. Boz Ratio Protocol (`F8`)
- **Mathematical Formulas**:
  $$\text{Dr. Boz Ratio (DBR)} = \frac{\text{Fasting Blood Glucose (mg/dL)}}{\text{Fasting Blood Ketones BHB (mmol/L)}} \qquad \Longleftrightarrow \qquad \text{DBR} = 18 \times \text{GKI}$$
- **Step-by-Step Morning Testing Protocol (`eHorWI3mvcU` @ `6:28` & `10:53`)**:
  1. Test **within 15–30 minutes of waking ("feet on the floor")**—strictly *before* coffee, showering, or exercise—using a calibrated dual capillary blood meter (**Keto-Mojo**).
  2. Lance the **lateral side of the ring or pinky fingertip** (where pain receptor density is lowest), wipe the first tiny drop if needed, and measure paired Glucose ($\text{mg/dL}$) and BHB ($\text{mmol/L}$).
  3. **Non-Negotiable Morning Floor**: Morning blood BHB must be **$\ge 0.7\text{ mmol/L}$** (never chronically sitting at $0.2\text{–}0.4\text{ mmol/L}$). Remember Dr. Boz's law: **when metabolism slips, ketones fall first and glucose rises second**.

| Dr. Boz Ratio (DBR) | Equivalent GKI ($\text{DBR} \div 18$) | Clinical & Metabolic Zone | Physiological State & Action Required |
|---|---|---|---|
| **$> 80$** | **$> 4.4$** | **Glucose-Burning / Stalled Zone** | Hepatic insulin remains too high; hormone-sensitive lipase is inhibited; fat stores are locked. Common in stalled carnivores (`eHorWI3mvcU`). **Action**: Eliminate dairy/late eating, compress to 16:8 2MAD, or run a 72h Sardine Fast. |
| **$40 \text{ – } 80$** | **$2.2 \text{ – } 4.4$** | **Nutritional Ketosis & Hypertrophy Zone** | **Target zone for Track 1 (Recomp) & Track 2 (Lean Bulk)**: Steady fat oxidation, clean cerebral BHB fuel, and optimal anabolic insulin/glucagon balance for heavy resistance training. |
| **$< 40$** | **$< 2.2$** | **Autophagy, Immune & Skin-Tightening Zone** | **AMPK exceeds mTOR**: Triggers cellular **autophagy** (recycling damaged organelles, tightening loose skin during major weight loss, resolving autoimmune inflammation, and repairing insulin receptors). |
| **$< 20$** | **$< 1.1$** | **Deep Therapeutic / Neuro-Regeneration Zone** | Intensive therapeutic ketosis achieved during 36h–72h fasts (`ketoCONTINUUM` Stages 9–12) for aggressive brain repair, mitophagy, and oncology support. |

### 4.6 The Ketovore Steak Molecular Cooking Protocol (`aTG5ttr57Ko` — 14-Chapter Culinary Science Breakdown) (`F8`, `F15`, `F16`)
Synthesized directly from Ryan Humiston's July 26, 2026 investigation **`aTG5ttr57Ko`** (*"The Science of Cooking the ULTIMATE Muscle Building Food (STEAK!)"*, `15:06`, 14 chapters) and Dr. Boz's whole-meat digestion chemistry (`eHorWI3mvcU`, `i3PP-ijOtXA`), how you salt and cook your ruminant beef directly governs its tenderness, water retention, lipid oxidation stability, and upper-GI amino acid absorption:

| Step / Variable | Chapter & Timestamp in `aTG5ttr57Ko` | Molecular & Thermodynamic Mechanism | Exact Ketovore Execution Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1. Cut Selection & Avoid "Blade-Tenderized / Meat-Glued" Cuts** | `1:20` What Doneness Actually Is<br>`3:04` Blade Tenderization & Transglutaminase<br>`3:57` Ribeye vs Sirloin<br>`5:18` USDA Meat Grades & Macros | Red liquid in raw steak is oxygen-binding **myoglobin** (not blood). Commercial mechanical blade tenderization pushes surface bacteria into the center of the cut, while transglutaminase ("meat glue") binds scrap trimmings. **Ribeye (USDA Prime or Choice)** provides the ideal $1:1$ to $1.3:1$ fat-to-protein gram ratio for Track 1/2/3 ketovore macros, while **Top Sirloin** serves as a leaner tool when adjusting fat macros. | Buy whole, non-blade-tenderized **Ribeye, NY Strip, or Chuck/Brisket**; match USDA grade (Prime vs Choice) to your Track's daily fat gram target. |
| **2. The `130°F` Protein Denaturation Bullseye (Myosin vs. Actin)** | `6:19` Myosin & Actin: The Two Proteins That Matter<br>`7:16` **Why 130°F Is the Bullseye** | Skeletal muscle meat is governed by two primary contractile proteins with vastly different thermal denaturation thresholds:<br>• **Myosin denatures at $\sim 122^\circ\text{F}$ ($50^\circ\text{C}$)**: Uncoiling myosin makes meat tender, buttery, and easily cleaved by gastric pepsin.<br>• **Actin denatures at $\sim 150^\circ\text{F}$ ($65.5^\circ\text{C}$)**: Once internal temperature crosses $145\text{–}150^\circ\text{F}$, actin filaments violently contract and wring out intracellular myoglobin water, turning steak tough, dry, and slow to digest. | Target a **final resting internal core temperature of exactly $130^\circ\text{F}$ ($54.4^\circ\text{C}$, Medium-Rare)** using an instant-read digital probe thermometer—100% of myosin is tenderized while **0% of actin is denatured**! |
| **3. Avoid the 10–40 Minute "Salt Dead Zone"** | `10:45` **The Salt Dead Zone** | When coarse salt hits raw beef, osmosis immediately draws intracellular moisture to the steak's surface.<br>• At **10 to 40 minutes**, surface water pools in a wet puddle that cannot evaporate in time—steaming the meat in the pan and ruining the Maillard crust (**The Salt Dead Zone**).<br>• After **$\ge 45\text{ minutes}$ (or 12–24h overnight uncovered dry-brine on a wire rack in the fridge)**, the concentrated brine dissolves surface myosin proteins and is **reabsorbed back into the muscle fibers**, desiccating the exterior pellicle for an explosive crust while seasoning the interior. | Salt your steak generously with Redmond Real Salt either:<br>• **Option A (Best)**: **$\ge 45\text{ minutes}$ to 24 hours in advance** (uncovered on a wire rack in the refrigerator), OR<br>• **Option B**: **Within $<3\text{ minutes}$** right before hitting the hot pan.<br>**NEVER salt 10–40 minutes before searing!** |
| **4. The Reverse Sear + High-Smoke-Point Animal Fat Sear** | `8:14` The Grey Band<br>`8:51` **The Reverse Sear**<br>`11:54` Cast Iron Thermal Mass<br>`12:28` Maillard, Oil & Smoke Points | Searing a cold thick steak from raw to $130^\circ\text{F}$ entirely in a pan overcooks the outer 30–40% beyond $150^\circ\text{F}$ (creating a tough, denatured **grey band**). Furthermore, searing in seed oils generates toxic oxidized aldehydes, while putting whole butter in a $500^\circ\text{F}$ pan burns milk solids (casein/lactose) at $350^\circ\text{F}$. | 1. **Low-Temp Oven/Smoker Warm-Up**: Place dry-brined steak in a **$225\text{–}250^\circ\text{F}$ oven** until internal temp reaches **$115^\circ\text{F}$** (activates natural cathepsin/calpain tenderizing enzymes and dries the surface).<br>2. **Cast-Iron Tallow/Ghee Sear**: Preheat a heavy **cast-iron skillet** (high thermal mass) until smoking hot with **$1\text{ tbsp}$ Beef Tallow or Clarified Ghee** (smoke point $420\text{–}485^\circ\text{F}$). Sear **45–60 seconds per side** for a glass-like Maillard crust, tossing in **$1\text{ tbsp}$ grass-fed butter + garlic/rosemary only in the final 20–30 seconds off-heat** to baste without burning milk solids! |

## PART 5: THE ULTIMATE SCIENCE-BACKED KETOVORE WORKOUT PROGRAM (`F9`)

### 5.1 The 3-Tier Session Architecture (Synthesizing Nippard + Ethier + Humiston for Ketosis) (`F9`)
Every workout in our blueprint is engineered around a **3-Tier Session Architecture** that maximizes mechanical tension and regional fiber recruitment while respecting ketogenic phosphagen recovery and preventing cortisol-driven gluconeogenesis:

| Tier | Creator Methodology | Volume Share | Rep Range & Tempo | Proximity to Failure (RIR) | Rest Interval | Physiological Purpose in Ketosis |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **TIER 1: Foundation** | **Jeff Nippard**<br>*(Heavy Multi-Joint S-Tier Compounds)* | **~50% of Session Sets** (2 exercises $\times$ 3 sets) | **5–8 or 6–10 Reps**<br>2–3s eccentric, 0.5–1s stretch pause, explosive concentric | **1–2 RIR** on Sets 1–2;<br>**0 RIR** on Last Set of safe machine/supported compounds | **2.5 – 3.0 Minutes** | Maximizes mechanical tension and progressive overload using the **ATP-PCr phosphagen system**, with 3-min rests allowing 90–95% aerobic $\text{PCr}$ resynthesis via fatty acids/BHB (Schoenfeld et al., 2016). |
| **TIER 2: Sculpting & Bi-Articulate Stretch** | **Jeremy Ethier & Jeff Nippard**<br>*(Sub-Region Cables, Bi-Articulate Stretch & `0ShG8aVIvms` S-Tier Abs)* | **~35% of Session Sets** (2–3 exercises $\times$ 2–3 sets) | **8–12 or 10–15 Reps**<br>3s eccentric, 1s pause in deep stretch, full ROM | **0–1 RIR** on early sets;<br>**0 RIR + 3–5 Lengthened Partials** on Final Set | **1.5 – 2.0 Minutes** | Aligns cable/resistance vectors with specific anatomical sub-regions (`Baf5YrNvEDU`) and stretches two-joint muscles across both joints (Maeo et al., 2021/2023; Wolf et al., 2025). |
| **TIER 3: Metabolic Finisher** | **Ryan Humiston**<br>*(Constant-Tension 3/4-ROM Isolation Burn)* | **~15% of Session Sets** (1 final set on 1–2 isolations) | **15–25 Reps**<br>Continuous 3/4 ROM (no joint lockout rest) | **True 0 RIR + 1 Mechanical Dropset or Rest-Pause** | **60 – 90 Seconds** (or end of muscle group) | Exhausts remaining high-threshold motor units via local intramuscular occlusion and cell swelling at the end of a muscle group's work **without** triggering systemic HPA-axis cortisol spillover. |

|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1. Chest (Clavicular / Upper)** | **30°–45° Incline Converging Machine or DB/Barbell Press** | **Low-to-High Cable Flye** (30° upward cable path) | **15° Low-Incline DB Press (3/4 Constant-Tension ROM)** | Tuck elbows at a **45°–60° arrow angle** (prevents anterior delt takeover); lower to a deep pectoral stretch with a 1s pause; drive biceps/elbows inward toward the sternum (Humiston internal adduction cue). |
| **2. Chest (Sternal & Costal)** | **Seated Cable Pec Flye** (bench set slightly forward for deep stretch) | **Flat Converging Machine Chest Press** or **Forward-Leaning Weighted Dips** | **Single-Arm Cross-Body Cable Press** (crossing midline) | On cable flyes, keep a slight elbow bend ("hug a barrel") and bring hands **across the midline** at peak contraction (`Baf5YrNvEDU` full ROM + squeeze). |
| **3. Lats (Vertical & Iliac)** | **Neutral-Grip / Close-Grip Lat Pulldown** or **Weighted Pull-Up** | **Half-Kneeling 1-Arm Sagittal Cable Lat Pull-Around** (iliac lower lats) | **Straight-Arm Cable Lat Prayer** (15–20 reps + 5 top-stretch partials) | Let shoulders elevate slightly at the top for a full lat stretch; depress scapulae and drive elbows tightly down along the ribcage arc toward the front hip pocket. |
| **4. Mid & Upper Back (Horizontal)** | **Chest-Supported T-Bar Row** or **Chest-Supported Machine Row** | **Deficit Pendlay Row** or **Seated Wide-Grip Cable Row** | **Incline Prone / Cable Y-Raise** (120–135° angle for lower traps) | Flare elbows at **45°–60°**; allow scapulae to **protract completely (round upper back slightly)** at the bottom stretch, then retract hard; add 3–5 lengthened partials after 0 RIR. |
| **5. Front Delts** | **Seated Machine Overhead Shoulder Press** | **Seated Dumbbell Shoulder Press** | **Humiston Reverse-Grip (Supinated) DB/Barbell Shoulder Press** | Front delts receive heavy stimulus from incline pressing; 2–3 direct sets/week suffices. Reverse grip tucks elbows into the scapular plane and spares the subacromial space. |
| **6. Side Delts** | **Wrist/Hip-Height Behind-the-Back Cuffed Cable Lateral Raise** (S+ Tier) | **Chest-Supported Lean-In DB Lateral Raise** or **Cable Y-Raise** | **Seated Slightly-Leaned-Back DB Lateral Raise** (20 reps constant tension) | Set cable pulley at **wrist/hip height** so peak torque hits the lateral deltoid in its cross-body stretched position; sweep arms out in the **30° scapular plane** ("reach for the walls, don't shrug"). |
| **7. Rear Delts** | **Reverse Cable Crossover** (unanchored cables crossed at shoulder height) | **Sideways 1-Arm Reverse Pec Deck** (reaching across torso) | **Humiston 20-Partial + 20-Full Rear Delt Cable/DB Flye** | Reach across the body at the start to pre-stretch the posterior deltoid; sweep the humerus back at a **~45° angle from the torso** where rear-delt leverage peaks. |
| **8. Biceps (Long Head, Short Head & Brachialis)** | **Bayesian Cable Curl** (facing away, wrist-height pulley, S+ Tier) | **45° Dumbbell / Machine Preacher Curl** & **Cross-Body Hammer Curl** | **Humiston Spider Curl** (elbows slightly forward) or **EZ-Bar Drag Curl** | **Bayesian Curl** extends the shoulder behind the torso to stretch the bi-articulate long head; **Preacher/Spider Curl** flexes the shoulder in front to isolate the short head and distal biceps with zero momentum. |
| **9. Triceps (Long, Lateral & Medial Heads)** | **Overhead Cable Triceps Extension** (rope or bar, S+ Tier) | **Incline EZ-Bar Skull Crusher** & **Cable Triceps Kickback / Pushdown** | **1-Arm Cross-Body Cable Extension** (15–20 reps constant tension) | **Overhead Extension** flexes the shoulder to maximally stretch the bi-articulate long head (~1.4x more growth, Maeo et al., 2023); **Cable Kickback** extends the shoulder to achieve peak shortened contraction. |
| **10. Quadriceps** | **Hack Squat** or **Pendulum Squat** (S+ Tier) | **Heel-Elevated Barbell Back Squat** & **Seated Leg Extension** (backrest reclined) | **Humiston 3/4-ROM Static Lunge** or **20-Rep Reclined Leg Extension Burn** | Descend to full knee flexion (hamstrings covering calves) with a 1s bottom pause; on **Seated Leg Extensions**, recline the seat back to stretch the bi-articulate **rectus femoris** across the hip joint. |
| **11. Hamstrings** | **Seated Leg Curl** (S+ Tier — ~1.5x growth vs Lying, Maeo 2021) | **Romanian Deadlift (RDL)** or **45° Hip-Biased Back Extension** | **Humiston Middle-80% Constant-Tension RDL** or **Dorsiflexed/Plantarflexed Leg Curl** | **Seated Leg Curl** flexes the hip at 90° to pre-stretch the hamstrings before knee flexion (lean torso slightly forward over the thigh pad); pair with **RDL** (knees soft at 15–20°, stopping just shy of top lockout). |
| **12. Glutes (Maximus & Medius)** | **Walking Dumbbell/Barbell Lunge** (long stride, 20° forward torso lean) | **Machine Hip Thrust** (mid-glutes) & **Seated Machine Hip Abduction** (upper glutes) | **Lean-Forward Seated Hip Abduction Burn** (20 reps + 10s iso-hold) | A **20° forward torso lean** on lunges/Bulgarian split squats shifts hip flexion torque onto the gluteus maximus stretch; leaning forward onSeated Hip Abduction targets the upper gluteus medius shelf. |
| **13. Calves & Abs/Core (`0ShG8aVIvms`)** | **Standing Straight-Knee Calf Raise** (S+ Tier)<br>**Face-Away Lat Pulldown Cable Crunch** (`0ShG8aVIvms` #1 Upper Abs) | **Leg Press Straight-Knee Toe Press**<br>**Garhammer Raises** (`0ShG8aVIvms` #1 Adv Lower Abs) or **AbMat Reverse Crunch** (#1 Beg Lower Abs) | **Standing Calf Raise Lengthened Partials** (5–8 bottom-half reps post-failure) | **Calves**: Straight knee stretches the bi-articulate gastrocnemius; **pause 2 full seconds** at bottom dorsiflexion to eliminate Achilles bounce.<br>**Abs (`0ShG8aVIvms`)**: Extend thoracic spine backward over lat pulldown pad on Face-Away Cable Crunches; curl pelvis into a **C-shape** from a hanging-hip stretch on Garhammer Raises. |

### 5.3 Full Program Option A: The 4-Day Upper/Lower Ketovore Hypertrophy Split (`F9`)
**Who Should Run Option A**: The **#1 recommended schedule for 85% of Ketovore lifters** (including Track 1 Recomposition, Track 3 Fat Loss, and busy professionals). Hits every muscle group **2x/week** with **10–14 hard sets/muscle/week**, leaving **3 full non-lifting days** (Wednesday, Saturday, Sunday) for mitochondrial recovery, Zone 2 cardio, and deep overnight ketogenesis.

#### Day 1 (Monday) — Upper Body A: Horizontal Push/Pull Priority + Bi-Articulate Arms + Upper Abs
| # | Exercise | Tier & Creator | Working Sets | Rep Range | Target RIR | Rest | Execution & Progression Cues |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **30°–45° Incline Converging Machine or Barbell/DB Press** | **Tier 1** (Nippard) | 3 | 6–8 | Sets 1–2: 1–2 RIR<br>Set 3: **0 RIR** (if machine/spotted) | 3.0 min | 45° elbow tuck; 2–3s eccentric; 1s pause on chest/bottom stretch; drive biceps inward toward sternum. |
| **2** | **Chest-Supported T-Bar Row or Machine Row** (Elbows 45°–60°) | **Tier 1** (Nippard/Ethier) | 3 | 8–10 | Sets 1–2: 1 RIR<br>Set 3: **0 RIR + 4–5 Lengthened Partials** | 2.5 min | Let scapulae protract fully at the bottom stretch; retract hard at peak; after Set 3 failure, perform 4–5 bottom-half reps. |
| **3** | **Neutral-Grip Lat Pulldown or Weighted Pull-Up** | **Tier 2** (Nippard/Ethier) | 3 | 8–10 | Sets 1–2: 1 RIR<br>Set 3: **0 RIR + 3–5 Lengthened Partials** | 2.0 min | Full overhead lat stretch at top; drive elbows down and in toward hips. |
| **4** | **Wrist-Height Behind-the-Back Cuffed Cable Lateral Raise** | **Tier 2** (Nippard/Ethier) | 3 | 10–15 | All sets: **0–1 RIR** (Set 3: **0 RIR + Partials**) | 90 sec | Cable crosses behind lower back at wrist height; sweep out in the 30° scapular plane. |
| **5** | **Overhead Cable Triceps Extension** (Rope or Straight Bar) | **Tier 2** (Nippard) | 3 | 10–12 | Sets 1–2: 1 RIR<br>Set 3: **0 RIR** | 90 sec | Step out facing away from low/mid pulley; let elbows flex fully behind head to stretch the bi-articulate long head. |
| **6** | **Bayesian Cable Curl** (2 sets) $\rightarrow$ **Humiston Spider Curl Finisher** (1 set) | **Tier 2 + Tier 3** (Nippard + Humiston) | 2 + 1 | Sets 1–2: 10–12<br>Set 3: **15–20** | Sets 1–2: **0 RIR**<br>Set 3: **0 RIR + Dropset** | 90 sec | Sets 1–2: Face away from low pulley, elbow pinned behind torso. Set 3: Chest on 45° incline bench, elbows slightly forward, 3/4 constant-tension burn. |
| **7** | **Face-Away Lat Pulldown Cable Crunch** (`0ShG8aVIvms` #1 Upper Abs) | **Tier 2** (Ethier S+ Tier) | 3 | 10–15 | All sets: **0 RIR** | 90 sec | Sit on lat pulldown seat facing *away* from stack, thigh pad raised against upper back; extend spine backward into full stretch, then crunch ribs to pelvis. |

### 5.4 Full Program Option B: The 5-Day Hybrid Upper/Lower (Strength) + Push/Pull/Legs (Hypertrophy) Split (`F9`)
**Who Should Run Option B**: Modeled directly on the **5-Day ULPPL Split** from Jeff Nippard & Dr. Stuart Phillips' 365-Day Experiment (`PiYSbR2B85w`) that produced **+10.0 lbs muscle / -10.0 lbs fat for Brad** and **+2.7 lbs pure retained muscle at 14.5% BF for advanced Jeff**. Best suited for **Track 2 (Lean Hypertrophy Surplus)** or well-recovered intermediate/advanced lifters with 8+ hours of sleep.

#### Weekly Schedule Overview
- **Monday**: Day 1 — **Upper Body (Strength & Heavy Mechanical Tension)**
- **Tuesday**: Day 2 — **Lower Body (Strength & Bi-Articulate Stretch)**
- **Wednesday**: **Rest & Active Mitochondrial Recovery** (8,000–12,000 steps + 30 min Zone 2)
- **Thursday**: Day 3 — **Push Hypertrophy (Chest, Side/Front Delts, Triceps + Upper Abs)**
- **Friday**: Day 4 — **Pull Hypertrophy (Lats, Mid-Back, Rear Delts, Biceps)**
- **Saturday**: Day 5 — **Legs Hypertrophy (Quads, Hamstrings, Glutes, Calves + Lower Abs)**
- **Sunday**: **Full Rest & Parasympathetic Recovery** (8,000–12,000 steps)

#### Complete 5-Day ULPPL Prescription Tables
| Day & Focus | Exercise Prescription (Sets $\times$ Reps @ Target RIR | Rest | Creator Cues) |
|---|---|
| **Day 1 (Mon): Upper Strength** | 1. **45° Incline Barbell or Converging Machine Bench Press** — $3 \times 5\text{–}7$ reps @ $1\text{–}2\text{ RIR}$ (Set 3: **0 RIR** w/ safeties, $3\text{ min}$ rest)<br>2. **Weighted Pull-Ups or Heavy Neutral-Grip Lat Pulldown** — $3 \times 6\text{–}8$ reps @ $1\text{ RIR}$ (Set 3: **0 RIR**, $3\text{ min}$ rest)<br>3. **Flat Dumbbell or Machine Chest Press** — $2 \times 6\text{–}8$ reps @ $1\text{ RIR}$ (Set 2: **0 RIR**, $2.5\text{ min}$ rest)<br>4. **Chest-Supported T-Bar Row or Deficit Pendlay Row** — $3 \times 6\text{–}8$ reps @ $1\text{ RIR}$ (Set 3: **0 RIR + 4 Lengthened Partials**, $2.5\text{ min}$ rest)<br>5. **Wrist-Height Cable Lateral Raise** — $3 \times 10\text{–}12$ reps @ **0 RIR** ($90\text{s}$ rest)<br>6. **Overhead Cable Triceps Extension** — $2 \times 8\text{–}10$ reps @ **0 RIR** ($90\text{s}$ rest)<br>7. **EZ-Bar or Dumbbell Preacher Curl** — $2 \times 8\text{–}10$ reps @ **0 RIR** ($90\text{s}$ rest) |
| **Day 2 (Tue): Lower Strength** | 1. **Hack Squat, Pendulum Squat, or Barbell Back Squat** — $3 \times 5\text{–}7$ reps @ $1\text{–}2\text{ RIR}$ (Set 3: **0 RIR** on Hack/Pendulum, $3\text{ min}$ rest)<br>2. **Barbell Romanian Deadlift (RDL)** — $3 \times 6\text{–}8$ reps @ $1\text{–}2\text{ RIR}$ ($3\text{ min}$ rest; deep hamstring stretch, no top lockout rest)<br>3. **Seated Leg Curl** — $3 \times 8\text{–}10$ reps @ $0\text{–}1\text{ RIR}$ (Set 3: **0 RIR + 5 Lengthened Partials**, $2\text{ min}$ rest)<br>4. **Reclined Seated Leg Extension** — $2 \times 10\text{–}12$ reps @ **0 RIR** ($90\text{s}$ rest)<br>5. **Standing Straight-Knee Calf Raise** — $3 \times 8\text{–}12$ reps @ **0 RIR** ($2\text{s}$ bottom stretch pause, $90\text{s}$ rest)<br>6. **Garhammer Raises** (Adv) or **Reverse Crunch with AbMat** (Beg) (`0ShG8aVIvms`) — $3 \times 10\text{–}15$ reps @ **0 RIR** ($90\text{s}$ rest) |
| **Day 3 (Thu): Push Hypertrophy + Upper Abs** | 1. **15°–30° Incline Dumbbell Press** — $3 \times 8\text{–}10$ reps @ $1\text{ RIR}$ (Set 3: **0 RIR**, $2.5\text{ min}$ rest)<br>2. **Seated Cable Pec Flye** (Deep Stretch + Midline Cross) — $2 \times 10\text{–}12$ reps @ **0 RIR** + **1 Humiston Finisher Set ($15\text{–}20$ reps constant tension)** ($90\text{s}$ rest)<br>3. **Seated Machine or Reverse-Grip Shoulder Press** — $2 \times 8\text{–}10$ reps @ $1\text{ RIR}$ (Set 2: **0 RIR**, $2\text{ min}$ rest)<br>4. **Behind-the-Back Cuffed Cable Lateral Raise** — $3 \times 12\text{–}15$ reps @ **0 RIR + Partials** ($90\text{s}$ rest)<br>5. **Overhead Cable Triceps Extension** — $2 \times 10\text{–}12$ reps @ **0 RIR** ($90\text{s}$ rest)<br>6. **Incline EZ-Bar Skull Crusher** (2 sets $\times 10\text{–}12$) + **1 Humiston Finisher Set: 1-Arm Cable Triceps Kickback ($15\text{–}20$ reps)** ($90\text{s}$ rest)<br>7. **Face-Away Lat Pulldown Cable Crunch** (`0ShG8aVIvms`) — $3 \times 10\text{–}15$ reps @ **0 RIR** ($90\text{s}$ rest) |
| **Day 4 (Fri): Pull Hypertrophy** | 1. **Wide-Grip or Neutral-Grip Lat Pulldown** — $3 \times 8\text{–}12$ reps @ $1\text{ RIR}$ (Set 3: **0 RIR + 4 Lengthened Partials**, $2\text{ min}$ rest)<br>2. **Chest-Supported Machine Row** (Scapular Protraction-to-Retraction) — $3 \times 8\text{–}12$ reps @ **0–1 RIR** ($2\text{ min}$ rest)<br>3. **Half-Kneeling 1-Arm Sagittal Cable Pull-Around** (Ethier Iliac Lats) — $2 \times 10\text{–}12$ reps/arm @ **0 RIR** ($90\text{s}$ rest)<br>4. **Reverse Cable Crossover** (Rear Delts) — $3 \times 12\text{–}15$ reps @ **0 RIR** (Set 3: **Humiston 20 Stretched Partials + 15 Full Reps**, $90\text{s}$ rest)<br>5. **Bayesian Cable Curl** (Long Head Stretch) — $3 \times 10\text{–}12$ reps @ **0 RIR** ($90\text{s}$ rest)<br>6. **Cross-Body Hammer Curl** (2 sets $\times 10\text{–}12$) + **1 Humiston Finisher Set: Spider Curl or EZ-Bar Drag Curl ($15\text{–}20$ reps)** ($90\text{s}$ rest) |
| **Day 5 (Sat): Legs Hypertrophy + Core** | 1. **Seated Leg Curl** (Done 1st per Nippard) — $3 \times 10\text{–}12$ reps @ **0 RIR + 5 Lengthened Partials** ($2\text{ min}$ rest)<br>2. **45° Leg Press or Belt/Pendulum Squat** — $3 \times 10\text{–}12$ reps @ $1\text{ RIR}$ (Set 3: **0 RIR**, 3/4 top ROM, $2.5\text{ min}$ rest)<br>3. **Walking Lunges or Bulgarian Split Squats** (20° Forward Torso Lean) — $3 \times 8\text{–}10$ reps/leg @ $1\text{ RIR}$ (Set 3: **0 RIR**, $2\text{ min}$ rest)<br>4. **Seated Machine Hip Abduction** (Leaning Forward) — $3 \times 12\text{–}15$ reps @ **0 RIR** ($90\text{s}$ rest)<br>5. **Reclined Seated Leg Extension** — $2 \times 12\text{–}15$ reps + **1 Humiston 20-Rep Finisher Set** ($90\text{s}$ rest)<br>6. **Leg Press Straight-Knee Toe Press** — $3 \times 12\text{–}15$ reps @ **0 RIR** ($2\text{s}$ stretch pause, $90\text{s}$ rest)<br>7. **Garhammer Raises / AbMat Reverse Crunch** (2 sets) + **Face-Away Lat Pulldown Cable Crunch** (2 sets) — $10\text{–}15$ reps @ **0 RIR** |

### 5.5 Progressive Overload, Autoregulation & Synchronized Deload + Autophagy Weeks (`F9`)
1. **Double-Progression Overload Rule**:
   - Every exercise has a prescribed rep range (e.g., $3 \times 8\text{–}10$ reps). Keep the weight fixed across weeks until you hit the **top of the rep range on all sets** at the prescribed RIR with strict 2–3s eccentric tempo and a 1s bottom pause.
   - At the next session, increase the weight by **$2.5\text{–}5\%$** ($2.5\text{–}5\text{ lbs}$ on cables/dumbbells, $5\text{–}10\text{ lbs}$ on heavy lower-body compounds) and build back up from the bottom of the rep range.
2. **Biomarker-Guided Autoregulation**:
   - If your **Morning Fasting Blood Test** shows an unexpected spike in fasting glucose ($>98\text{ mg/dL}$), a drop in morning BHB ($<0.5\text{ mmol/L}$), or an Oura/wearable sleep score drop after a poor night's sleep, **do not add extra glycolytic sets**—drop working sets by 1 set per exercise (e.g., 2 sets instead of 3), extend rest intervals to 3 full minutes, and prioritize an early dinner cutoff.
3. **The 7-Week Mesocycle: Synchronizing Training Deloads with 36–72h Autophagy Fasts**:
   - **Weeks 1–6 (Accumulation & Progressive Overload)**: Train hard on Option A (4-Day) or Option B (5-Day) while eating 2MAD inside your goal Track.
   - **Week 7 (Synchronized Deload + Autophagy Week)**:
     - **Days 1–3 of Week 7 (Optional 36-Hour Monk Fast or 72-Hour Autophagy / Sardine Fast)**: Perform 1 full-body or upper/lower maintenance session on **Day 1 of the fast (Hours 12–18)** to deplete liver glycogen and launch BHB $>2.0\text{ mmol/L}$. During **Hours 24–72** (when AMPK is maximally activated and **Dr. Boz Ratio drops $<20$**), do **only Zone 2 walking and mobility**—no eccentric muscle damage while mTORC1 is suppressed.
     - **Days 4–7 of Week 7 (Deload Refeed & mTORC1 Rebound)**: Break the fast gently (bone broth + 2 eggs, followed 60 min later by a normal ketovore meal) and perform 2 light Deload Workouts (**50% normal set volume, -10–15% load, stopping at 3–4 RIR**). By Monday of the next mesocycle, joints are healed, cellular grime is recycled via autophagy, insulin sensitivity is razor-sharp, and muscle tissue is primed for **post-fasting mTORC1 supercompensation**!

### 5.5.1 Active Joint & Thoracic Mobility Inside Training ("Neural Neurotone" `sC91H2XLtJc`), Fixing Muscular "Leaks" (`jrkdKbcUUnc`), Banded Joint Traction (`WWEYP0CFmRQ`), and the McGill Big 3 Spine Protocol (`BqL-_eOiOKw`, `bcbuhePZZj0`) (`F9`, `F14`, `F15`, `F16`)
Synthesizing Ryan Humiston's physical-therapy/biomechanics series (`sC91H2XLtJc`, `WWEYP0CFmRQ`, `Y7hD07u6-zg`, `WAuPlk_2naI`, `jrkdKbcUUnc`, `mKUFh_DvDRo`, `wzcH33QiRwA`) with Jeremy Ethier's collaborations with spine biomechanist **Dr. Stuart McGill** (`BqL-_eOiOKw`, `bcbuhePZZj0`, `ChLmR-1UBMI`, `rsxKcJVTtUw`) and tendon rehabilitation (`GSwL3Lgw6zI`):
1. **Replace Static Floor Stretching with Loaded Eccentric Mobility ("Neural Neurotone" — `sC91H2XLtJc`)**:
   - Chronic joint tightness is rarely short fascia; it is protective CNS **"neural neurotone"** (`sC91H2XLtJc` @ `1:15`) guarding a weak end-range joint angle. ### 5.6 Cardio & Conditioning Integration (`F9`)
1. **Daily NEAT Anchor (8,000–12,000 Steps/Day)**: Non-negotiable baseline (Jeremy Ethier). Include a **10–15 minute brisk walk immediately after Meal 1 and Meal 2** to clear postprandial lipids and glucose via insulin-independent contraction-mediated GLUT4 uptake.
2. **Zone 2 Mitochondrial Conditioning (2–3x / Week for 30–45 Minutes)**: Perform on non-lifting days (or after lifting, never immediately before heavy squats) at **60–70% $\text{HR}_{\text{max}}$** ($\sim 180 - \text{Age}$ bpm, strict nasal breathing). Upregulates mitochondrial density and capillary beds so your muscles regenerate $\text{PCr}$ faster between lifting sets.
3. **Optional Alactic / Phosphagen Sprints (1x / Week Max)**: Avoid 45-minute high-lactate HIIT bootcamps (which spike cortisol and hepatic gluconeogenesis). If performing sprint work, use **6–8 rounds of 8–10 second all-out bike/hill sprints** (pure ATP-PCr phosphagen system) with **2.5–3 minutes of slow walking recovery** between sprints.
4. **High Energy Flux (`A9pEtDm2nbs`, `BL3vZdk61to`) & The Cold Plunge / Ice Bath Hypertrophy Rule (`IYni6AfrkAY`)**:
   - **High Energy Flux Over Starvation Dieting (`A9pEtDm2nbs` @ `5:10`, `BL3vZdk61to`)**: Rather than slashing calories to starvation levels while sedentary (which downregulates thyroid $T_3$ and resting metabolic rate), maintain **High Energy Flux**—walking **8,000–12,000+ daily steps** while eating abundant whole animal protein and fat ($1.6\text{–}2.2\text{ g/kg}$ protein).
   - **Strict Cold Plunge Timing Rule (`IYni6AfrkAY` @ `0:34`; Roberts et al., 2015 *J Physiol*)**: While deliberate cold exposure ($50\text{–}59^\circ\text{F}$ for $2\text{–}3\text{ min}$) activates **brown adipose tissue (BAT) UCP1 thermogenesis** (`IYni6AfrkAY` @ `1:02`, `rz8gbiNTLYg`) and elevates baseline dopamine for hours (`2:50`, `fxICyViHCSw`), **never take an ice bath or cold plunge within 4 hours AFTER resistance training**—acute post-workout cold vasoconstriction shuts down intramuscular amino acid delivery, suppresses satellite cell activation, and blunts **mTORC1 myofibrillar protein synthesis**. Schedule cold plunges **strictly on non-lifting rest days or in the morning $\ge 4\text{–}6\text{ hours}$ BEFORE lifting**.
